# SCRIPTS.md: 190 ready-to-produce scripts

**Original library (S01–S60):** 30 Chang Yin (S01–S30) · 20 Sun Yoon (S31–S50) · 10 Duo (S51–S60), each 20–45 s.  
**Blitz expansion (S61–S150):** 90 scripts written to POSTDB_FINDINGS §8: visible physical demos of 30–59 s (70–130 spoken words), the object or body part in the first three words, the 'If you [habit] every [time]' / myth / 'watch what happens' grammars, the US kitchen-item series (Kitchen Gym #1–3, Sun Checks Your Kitchen #1–16), tests the viewer does right now, Sun's public debunks of viral remedies, share triggers and a ~3-second skip/safety line in every script. **S135–S150 are the founding launch week** (doors-open, pinned-post variants, gift-for-parents, challenge kickoffs) built for the charge-today founding offer (BLITZ.md).  
**Organic runway + launch week (S151–S190, CANON UPDATE 2):** 25 value-first runway scripts (free WAITLIST keyword in 8; the rest route to the waitlist through runway-mode DM flows) and 15 launch-week scripts for the Shopify ebook front end (BOOK, `{{EBOOK_PRICE}}` one-time) and the founding membership (post-purchase offer or JOIN; 'locked for as long as you stay subscribed'). Also rendered on their own in RUNWAY_SCRIPTS.md.  
**By page:** @changyin 48 · @sunyoon.kitchen 46 · @changandsun 39 · @changyin.mobility 23 · @changyin.strength 17 · @sunyoon 17. Coverage tables: SCRIPTS_COVERAGE.md.

Machine-readable copy: `data/content/scripts.json` (CHARACTERS.md §12.4 schema, plus optional v2 keys `hook_grammar`, `series`, `launch_week`, `prop`, `skip_line`). Source: `data/content/scripts_*.py` and `scripts_launch.py`, rebuilt and validated with `python3 tools/build_content.py`.

**Offer placeholders in launch scripts:** `{{FOUNDING_PRICE}}` (the live founding price from the $25/$30 split, one value per render) and `{{DOMAIN}}`. The 5,000 founding cap is real and publicly counted; no script states a 'spots left' number (SAFETY T-04). Every JOIN script states price, monthly renewal, cancel online anytime and the 14-day money-back (SAFETY S-02).

**Production notes that apply to every script**
- Burned-in `AI character` corner tag on every frame (SAFETY D-02). The caption footer and the movement add-on are auto-appended (SAFETY §7). They're **not** repeated in the captions below.
- Shot strings: `SET-CODE | wardrobe | action | camera`. Sets and wardrobe codes are in CHARACTERS.md §4.5, §5.5 and §6. Every movement shot passes form QC (SAFETY M-08).
- Study-card insets use the house card design with the real citation (SAFETY V-03), lower-left, 2 s.
- On-screen text: max 2 lines, 52–64 px, white on a dark pill or near-black on cream. **No gray text.**
- Word counts are spoken words. At ~140 wpm (Chang) / ~155 wpm (Sun) plus demo pauses, S01–S60 land in 20–45 s and S61–S150 in 30–59 s (the return-era sweet spot, POSTDB §3b).

**Page** @changyin · **Speaker** CHANG · **Format** F02 · **Pillar** P01 (Strength proof & tests) · **Hook** H031 · **Target** 44 s · **Spoken words** 91 · **CTA** `STRONG` → 8-Minute Chair Builder

**Hook line:** "If you use your hands every time you stand, try this. Thirty seconds. No hands."

**Virality (VIRALITY_SYSTEM.md §2):** 77.0 · gate PASS · hook class IF_EVERY · needs: no open loop / re-hook by second 3–6; no share trigger ('send this to…', 'do it with your…'; shares rank first, POSTDB §3c); no save trigger (a routine / day N / tonight / write it down)

| 3-9 | CHANG | Chair against the wall. Arms crossed on your chest. Feet flat. | Chair AGAINST the wall | same · CU chair legs touching wall, then feet flat · low angle CU |
| 9-20 | CHANG | Go. All the way up. All the way down. Breathe out as you stand. | Breathe out as you stand | same · Chang does fast smooth reps; on-screen counter and 30 s timer · medium-full side 3/4 |

| 26-35 | CHANG | Men 70 to 74, under twelve is below average. Women, under ten. That's the below-average line in the standard test. | Men 70–74: under 12 · Women: under 10 | same · study card inset lower-left: CDC STEADI · medium, card inset 2 s |

**Full spoken script (91 words):** If you use your hands every time you stand, try this. Thirty seconds. No hands. Chair against the wall. Arms crossed on your chest. Feet flat. Go. All the way up. All the way down. Breathe out as you stand. I'm 74. I get twenty-two. Your number is the one that matters. Men 70 to 74, under twelve is below average. Women, under ten. That's the below-average line in the standard test. Need your hands? Use them. Score zero. Start today anyway. Comment STRONG. I'll send you the 8-minute chair builder.

**Safety cue:** Chair against the wall (spoken + OST). Breathe out as you stand. Caption add-on: stop if chest pain, dizziness or sharp pain.  
**Regression:** Hands on thighs (Frank); higher seat with a firm cushion.  
**Movement tags:** sit_to_stand

```
Comment STRONG for the 8-minute Chair Builder.

Below average at 70–74: men under 12, women under 10. It's a sign worth working on, not a diagnosis.
```

I'll send you the Steady Feet routine.

**Safety cue:** Counter within reach, fingers hovering (spoken + OST). Touching is allowed. Caption add-on stop rule.

**Page** @changyin · **Speaker** CHANG · **Format** F02 · **Pillar** P01 (Strength proof & tests) · **Hook** H033 · **Target** 44 s · **Spoken words** 103 · **CTA** `TEST` → Strength Age test (quiz)

**Hook line:** "Sit on the floor and get up without hands. Score yourself out of ten."

**Virality (VIRALITY_SYSTEM.md §2):** 81.1 · gate PASS · hook class COMMAND · needs: no share trigger ('send this to…', 'do it with your…'; shares rank first, POSTDB §3c)

| Time (s) | Speaker | Spoken | On-screen text | Shot (set · wardrobe · action · camera) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0-3 | CHANG | Sit on the floor and get up without hands. Score yourself out of ten. | THE FLOOR TEST | SET-LIVING · C-CASUAL · Chang standing on rug-free wood floor beside sturdy sofa · eye-level wide |
| 3-9 | CHANG | Safety first. Sturdy sofa next to you. Someone home. New hip or knee? Skip this one. | Sofa next to you · someone home | same · taps sofa arm · medium |
| 9-19 | CHANG | Start at ten. Cross your feet. Sit down. Every hand, knee or forearm you use costs one point. | 10 points · minus 1 per support | same · crosses feet, lowers to cross-legged sit, rises without hands · wide, label 'Chang's level, not your starting point' |
| 19-27 | CHANG | In a study of 2,002 adults aged 51 to 80, people who got up off the floor more easily tended to have longer lives. | 2,002 adults · 51–80 · every point counts | same · study card inset (Brito/Araújo) · medium |
| 27-35 | CHANG | Frank used a hand and a knee to get up. Minus two. Eight. Good. Now we know. | Frank: 8. Still counts. | same · Frank gets up with a hand on the sofa and a knee, laughs · medium two-shot |

**Full spoken script (103 words):** Sit on the floor and get up without hands. Score yourself out of ten. Safety first. Sturdy sofa next to you. Someone home. New hip or knee? Skip this one. Start at ten. Cross your feet. Sit down. Every hand, knee or forearm you use costs one point. In a study of 2,002 adults aged 51 to 80, people who got up off the floor more easily tended to have longer lives. Frank used a hand and a knee to get up. Minus two. Eight. Good. Now we know. Write it down. Test again next month. Comment TEST for the full strength check.

**Safety cue:** Sturdy sofa next to you, someone home; skip if new hip/knee (spoken). Stand slowly afterward (caption). Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Chair version: sit to stand without hands first; floor practice only with sofa support (see S18).  
**Movement tags:** floor_transfer

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment TEST and we'll send the free Strength Age check.
The sitting-rising test: from standing, sit on the floor and get back up. Start at 10, lose 1 point for each hand, knee or forearm used.
In 2,002 adults aged 51–80, people who scored higher, getting up with less help, tended to have longer lives. Only try it with a sturdy sofa beside you and someone home. New hip or knee: skip it.
```

| 3-11 | CHANG | Healthy people, average age 67. Ten days of bed rest. Almost a kilo from the legs. Younger people took 28 days to lose less than half that. | Age 67: 10 days · Age 38: 28 days | same · study card inset (Kortebein, JAMA 2007) · medium |

**Full spoken script (102 words):** If you spend ten days in bed, watch what happens to your legs. Healthy people, average age 67. Ten days of bed rest. Almost a kilo from the legs. Younger people took 28 days to lose less than half that. Sick or recovering? If your doctor says move, move a little every day. In bed, squeeze your thighs for ten seconds, ten times. Breathe through it. Ankle pumps. Then sit on the edge and stand up five times, hands on your thighs. Just had surgery? Follow your own team's plan first. Muscle is a savings account. Comment STRONG for the chair builder.

**Safety cue:** Doctor approval when recovering; surgery = follow your team's plan. Stand holding the bedside. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Thigh squeezes and ankle pumps only (no standing) until cleared.  
**Movement tags:** sit_to_stand, isometric_hold, standing_up_fast

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment STRONG for the 8-minute Chair Builder.
In healthy adults (average age 67), 10 days of bed rest cost ~0.95 kg of leg lean mass. Younger adults lost under 0.4 kg over 28 days.
If you're sick or recovering and your doctor says it's OK, small daily movement adds up: thigh squeezes (breathe through them), ankle pumps, a few careful stands, breathing out as you rise. High blood pressure or a heart condition? Ask your doctor before long holds.
```

```
Comment BALANCE for Steady Feet.
The U.S. Preventive Services Task Force gives exercise programs a Grade B recommendation for adults 65+ living at home, and recommends against vitamin D supplements for the same goal. A Cochrane review pooled 108 trials of balance and strength programs like these three moves. Supplements don't replace food or medicine. Ask your doctor or pharmacist, especially if you take prescriptions.
If your doctor prescribed vitamin D for another reason, keep following their advice.
```

```
Comment BREATH for the 4-6 Breath practice.
In a Stanford study (Balban et al., 2023), 111 adults practiced 5 minutes a day for a month. Cyclic sighing (two inhales through the nose, long exhale through the mouth) produced the biggest daily improvement in positive mood, more than mindfulness meditation.
No breath holds. If you feel lightheaded, go back to normal breathing.
```

Only as far as comfortable. Now left. Ten times. Slow. This isn't a stretching contest. Now slide one heel down until the leg is straight, then back up. Ten times. Other leg. Hands on your belly. **Safety cue:** Rotation 'only as far as comfortable'; sit 10 s before standing (spoken). Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Smaller knee range; skip heel slides if hips are sore.

```
90 seconds, lying down: gentle knee rocks, heel slides, then slow breathing (in 4, out 6). Slow breathing increases heart-rate variability, the calm side of your nervous system.
Osteoporosis: keep the knee rocks small and comfortable. Getting up at night? Sit for 10 seconds first.
```

**Full spoken script (98 words):** If you get up every night to use the bathroom, sit and count to ten first. Stand up fast and your blood pressure can dip for a moment. In older adults it's common. About one in five. Rule one: sit on the edge, pump your ankles ten times, count to ten. Rule two: stand holding the bed, count to five. Rule three: a night light on the path. Dizzy every time you stand? Tell your doctor. Some medicines do this. Don't change them yourself. Send this to someone who gets up at night. Comment SLEEP for the wind-down.

**Safety cue:** Explicit orthostatic protocol; medication = doctor, never self-adjust.  
**Regression:** If dizzy standing, sit back down and call for help; keep a phone by the bed.  
**Movement tags:** standing_up_fast

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment SLEEP for the Sleep Wind-Down.
A blood-pressure drop when standing (orthostatic hypotension) is common in older adults, roughly 1 in 5 in community studies. At night: sit, pump your ankles, count to 10. Stand holding the bed. Light the path.
If you're dizzy every time you stand, tell your doctor. Don't change your medicines on your own.
```

**Full spoken script (84 words):** Curl-ups for a strong core? Not for older spines. Do this instead. If your bones might be thinning, bone experts say skip repeated forward bending with load. Instead: hands on the counter. Walk your feet back. Body straight like a board. Breathe. Fifteen seconds. Then march slowly. One knee. Other knee. Then carry a grocery bag in one hand. Walk tall. Don't lean. That's core too. Wrists or shoulders complain? Use the wall instead of the counter. Comment BACK. I'll send you Morning Unlock.

**Safety cue:** Loaded spinal flexion explicitly avoided (E40); breathe during plank; wall regression. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Wall plank (more upright), 10 s.  
**Movement tags:** plank_incline, carry

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment BACK for Morning Unlock.
Osteoporosis exercise guidance (Too Fit To Fracture) recommends avoiding repeated or loaded forward spinal bending like curl-ups. Swap in counter planks, slow marches and one-hand carries.
Known osteoporosis or a past spine fracture? Ask your doctor or a physio for your plan.
```

```
Comment BACK for Morning Unlock.
The hip hinge uses your hips, not your low back, to bend and lift. Broom on your back (head, upper back and tailbone touching), hips back, soft knees, stand by squeezing your glutes. Keep loads close.
Exercise is one of the best-studied approaches to chronic low back pain (Cochrane, 249 trials). Mild discomfort up to 3 out of 10 is okay if it settles by tomorrow; sharper or worse the next day, back off. Pain shooting down the leg or numbness: see your doctor.
```

**Full spoken script (101 words):** If you fell right now, could you get up? Practice before you need it. Practice with a sturdy chair and someone home. Hurt after a real fall? Don't get up. Call for help. The way up. Roll to your side. Onto hands and knees. Crawl to the chair. Hands on the seat. One foot forward, like a knight kneeling. Push through that front foot. Turn and sit. Sit a moment, then stand slowly. Can't get down safely yet? Practice only the last part. Kneel on a cushion by the chair, and stand. Earn the floor. Comment STRONG for the chair builder.

**Safety cue:** Someone home, sturdy chair; injured after fall = don't get up, call; backward-chaining regression. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Backward chaining: half-kneel to stand from a cushion with chair support.  
**Movement tags:** floor_transfer, standing_up_fast

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment STRONG for the 8-minute Chair Builder.
Getting up from the floor is a skill you can practice (floor-rise strategy training in older adults). Side → hands and knees → crawl to a sturdy chair → half-kneel → push up through the front foot → turn and sit.
Practice only with someone home. Sit a moment in the chair, then stand slowly. If you're hurt after a real fall, don't get up: call for help. New hip or knee: follow your surgeon's precautions.
```

Fix one: open the cabinet door. Put one foot on the bottom shelf. Switch feet every few minutes. Fix two: soft knees, hips back a little, and step closer to the sink. Fix three: every ten minutes, stand tall, hands on your low back, lean back gently. Three times, only if it feels good. Or he could do the dishes more often. We have a dishwasher. It's me. Comment BACK for Morning Unlock.

**Safety cue:** Extension only if comfortable; caption add-on stop rule; leg pain/numbness → doctor in caption.  
**Regression:** Skip extension if it causes leg symptoms; sit on a stool for long tasks.

```
Sink-back fixes: one foot on the open cabinet shelf (switch often), soft knees with your hips slightly back, and a gentle stand-tall lean back every 10 minutes if it feels good.
Mild discomfort up to 3 out of 10 is okay if it settles by tomorrow; sharper, or worse the next day, back off. Long-term, exercise is one of the best-studied approaches to chronic low back pain (Cochrane). Pain down the leg, numbness or weakness: see your doctor.
```

Ten push-ups on the back of the bench. Keep walking. Bench wobbly or wet? Skip it. Use your chair at home. Comment STRONG for the 8-minute chair builder.

```
Add a bench stop to your walk.
```

**Full spoken script (105 words):** Knee hurts, so you stopped walking. Now it hurts more. Break the loop. Muscles around the knee share the load. In trials, strength training eased arthritis knee pain a little, and made chairs a lot easier. Level one: breathe out and stand up from a high chair, ten times. Level two: same, lower chair. Level three: bottom-stair step-ups, holding the rail. Move up when ten feel easy for three days. Pain rule: up to three out of ten is okay if it's calm by morning. Worse the next day? Back down a level. Knee hot and swollen? Doctor first. Comment KNEES for the Step Builder.

**Safety cue:** Pain-monitoring rule, level-down rule, hot swollen joint red flag, rail support. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Level 1 is the regression; add a cushion for a higher seat.

```
Knee ladder: L1 stand from a high chair ×10 → L2 lower chair → L3 bottom-stair step-ups holding the rail. Level up after 3 easy days.
In the Cochrane review of 121 trials, strength training improved chair rising and modestly reduced osteoarthritis pain. Pain rule: ≤3/10 that settles by morning is OK. A hot, swollen knee needs a doctor.
```

| 3-12 | CHANG | Strength training in older adults made getting out of a chair a lot easier, and eased arthritis knee pain a little. Tai chi matched physical therapy for knee arthritis in 204 people. | Chair rise ↑ · OA pain ↓ · tai chi ≈ PT | same · two study cards inset · medium |
| 12-25 | CHANG | The trick is depth you own. Squat to a chair. Touch it lightly. Breathe out and stand. Knees follow your toes, not inward. | Tap the chair · knees follow toes | same · CU knees tracking over toes; bad version knees caving with ✗ · CU + medium |

**Full spoken script (89 words):** Squats are bad for older knees? The research disagrees. Strength training in older adults made getting out of a chair a lot easier, and eased arthritis knee pain a little. Tai chi matched physical therapy for knee arthritis in 204 people. The trick is depth you own. Squat to a chair. Touch it lightly. Breathe out and stand. Knees follow your toes, not inward. Chair too low? Add a cushion. Go lower only when it's easy. Knee replacement? Your surgeon's rules come first. Comment KNEES for the Step Builder.

**Safety cue:** Controlled depth to chair; knee tracking; replacement precautions. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Higher seat with cushion; hands on thighs.  
**Movement tags:** squat_to_chair

```
Comment KNEES for the Step Builder.
In older adults, strength training substantially improved chair rising and modestly reduced osteoarthritis pain (Cochrane, 121 trials). In a 204-person trial, tai chi matched physical therapy for knee osteoarthritis over a year (Annals of Internal Medicine 2016).
Squat to a chair you can own. Knees follow toes. New knee: follow your surgeon.
```


**Full spoken script (91 words):** When we fight, one of us says 'exhale,' in for four, out for six. Here's why it works. A long exhale works like a brake. **Safety / cautions:** Crisis resource in caption (relationship conflict topic); no breath holds.

```
Comment BREATH for the 4-6 Breath.
A meta-analysis (Laborde 2022) found voluntary slow breathing increases vagally mediated heart-rate variability, a marker of the calming branch of the nervous system. Chang and Sun's rule: in 4, out 6, three times, then talk.
If arguments at home ever make you feel unsafe, in the US call 1-800-799-7233.
```

**Full spoken script (96 words):** Your heart's best friend isn't only the treadmill. Not only the bike. Muscles get a vote. Add walking, more so. So: chair stands, counter push-ups, carries. Breathe out on the hard part. Never strain with a held breath. Heart condition, chest pain, or new breathlessness? Get cleared by your doctor first. Keep the walks. Add the muscle. Comment STRONG for the chair builder.

```
The AHA's 2023 scientific statement recommends resistance training for adults with and without cardiovascular disease, and warns against breath-holding. In a BJSM 2022 meta-analysis, adults doing 30–60 min/week of muscle-strengthening activity tended to have longer lives, more so with aerobic activity added (observational).
Heart condition or chest symptoms? Get individual clearance first.
```

```
Clear the rugs, shoes on, wall beside you.
```

**Full spoken script (97 words):** The number one thing to test is not thigh size, not the scale. It's strength. Around 75, muscle size drops about one percent a year. Strength drops two and a half to four. Two to five times faster. So the mirror lies. Test what your legs can do, not how they look. Train it: chair against the wall. Stand up with some speed. Sit down slowly, three seconds. Breathe out as you stand. Six reps. Joints cranky? Take the speed out. Slow both ways is still training. Measure twice. Lift once. Comment STRONG for the chair builder.

**Safety cue:** Chair against the wall; breathe out; slow option for cranky joints. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Slow both ways, hands on thighs.  
**Movement tags:** sit_to_stand, power

```
Comment STRONG for the 8-minute Chair Builder.
Around age 75, muscle mass drops about 0.6–1% a year while strength drops about 2.5–4% a year (Frontiers in Physiology 2012), so strength fades 2–5× faster than size. Test and train what your legs can do: chair against a wall, stand with some speed, lower for 3 seconds, breathe out as you stand.
Cranky joints: slow both ways.
```

**Full spoken script (101 words):** Soup cans. Curl them slowly, and just watch my forearms work. About a pound each. Light is fine. Muscle answers effort, and you build up. Move one: stand at the counter, arms at your sides, curl up slow, lower slower. Ten. Move two: raise them forward to shoulder height, only as high as comfortable. Breathe out up. Eight. Move three: squeeze them to your chest and do five chair stands. Chair against the wall. In 121 trials, strength training improved strength a lot in older adults. Soup counts. Shoulder pain lifting forward? Skip move two. Comment STRONG for the chair builder.

**Safety cue:** Only to comfortable height (overhead_press_loaded rule); chair against the wall; breathe out up; shoulder skip line. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Cans only, no chair stands; or seated curls.

```
Shoulder pain lifting forward? Skip the raises.
```

**Full spoken script (90 words):** If you shuffle every time you walk, try fast feet at the counter. Three each way. Strength is how much. Power is how fast. Training with fast intent works about as well as regular lifting, sometimes better. Hands on the counter. Quick side steps. Three left. Three right. Stay light. Breathe. Then quick heel raises. Ankle alphabet + heel-toe rocks while the coffee brews. Calf muscles help push blood back toward the heart (the calf pump). Orthostatic hypotension, a blood-pressure drop on standing, affects roughly 22% of community-dwelling older adults: sit, pump your ankles, then stand slowly holding something.
```
One calf red, swollen and painful? That's for your doctor, not exercise.
```

```
Hot swollen knee or night pain that won't settle: see your doctor, no exercise.
```

**Full spoken script (89 words):** Drinking straw. Breathe out through it slowly. Watch what happens to my shoulders. The straw makes your exhale long. Long exhales slow your breathing down to about six a minute. Slow breathing like that raises heart-rate variability, a sign your calming system is working. That's from a big review. Try it. In through your nose, four. Out through the straw, six. Again. Again. No holding. Lightheaded? Stop, breathe normally. Lung condition? Ask your doctor about breathing drills. Keep the straw by the kettle. Comment BREATH for the 4-6 breath.

**Safety / cautions:** No breath retention (E42); lightheadedness stop line; lung-condition line.

**Caption** (footer auto-appended):
```
Comment BREATH for the 5-minute 4-6 Breath.
A straw lengthens your exhale. Slow breathing around 6 breaths a minute increases vagally mediated heart-rate variability during and after practice (Laborde et al., 2022 meta-analysis). In 4 through the nose, out 6 through the straw, no holding.
```

```
New leg numbness or weakness, or bladder/bowel changes: see a doctor today.
```

```
Comment SOUP for Sun Yoon's Three Soups.
Warm water on an empty stomach is just water. There's no competent evidence it 'changes your body'. What has a consensus behind it: PROT-AGE suggests about 25–30 g protein per meal for adults over 65. Two eggs (~12 g), a cup of soy milk (~7 g) and ½ cup edamame (~9 g) gets close to 30.
Kidney disease or a fluid limit? Follow your doctor's numbers.
```

```
Never honey for babies under 1. Diabetes: honey counts as sugar.
```

```
Go up slowly and drink water. On a restricted kidney diet? Ask your dietitian.
```

**Full spoken script (99 words):** If you jump up from the couch every time the show ends, the room spins. A blood-pressure drop when you stand up is common. One review found it in about 22 percent of older adults at home. The rule: scoot to the edge. Pump your ankles ten times. Breathe out and stand, hand on the couch arm. Count to five before you walk. Comment BALANCE for Steady Feet.

```
Comment BALANCE for Steady Feet.
The couch rule: scoot to the edge, 10 ankle pumps, stand with a hand on the couch arm, count to five before walking. Orthostatic hypotension, a blood-pressure drop on standing, was found in about 22% of community-dwelling older adults in a meta-analysis; some medicines raise the risk. Don't change them; tell your doctor.
Fainted or hit your head? Get checked today.
```

**Full spoken script (105 words):** Two chairs. Seven days. Ten minutes. Loser does dishes all week. Day one is today on my page. We're doing it together. You can too. Partner, sister, neighbor. In 121 trials, older adults training two or three times a week got better at rising from a chair. Need your hands? Hands on your thighs. It still counts. Chest pain or dizziness? Stop, and call your doctor. Type your two numbers below. Comment STRONG for the chair builder. Chang: fourteen dishes.

**Safety cue:** Chairs against the wall; breathe out; hands-on-thighs regression; red-flag stop line. Caption add-on stop rule.  
**Regression:** Hands on thighs; firm cushion on the seat.

```
Chest pain or dizziness: stop and call your doctor.
```

```
Comment SOUP for Sun Yoon's Three Soups.
The famous chicken-soup study (Rennard et al., Chest 2000) was in a lab dish: soup slowed white-blood-cell movement, and the authors said benefit in people remains untested. Make it because it's warm, easy and carries protein.
Watching salt? Use half. Fever over 3 days or trouble breathing? Call your doctor.
```

```
Seaweed iodine varies enormously: commercially available seaweeds measured 16 to 2,984 mcg iodine per gram. The adult upper limit is 1,100 mcg/day from all sources. A snack pack is fine; a daily handful of packs isn't the same thing.
Thyroid condition or thyroid medicine? Ask your doctor before eating seaweed daily.
```
